			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Automating customer retention workflows in Amazon Quick</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/automating-customer-retention-workflows-in-amazon-quick/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Vaidy Janardhanam]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 29 Jul 2026 15:24:21 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Quick Suite]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Simple Storage Service (S3)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[How-To]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">feb5d9d0ff41eed6fe60095636b3550675d64da2</guid>

					<description>Learn how to build a no-code customer retention pipeline in Amazon Quick that detects at-risk customers from call transcripts and CSAT data, scores them by retention priority with a custom MCP Action, and generates personalized retention letters, reducing response time from days to minutes.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Automating customer retention workflows in Amazon Quick can turn a five-day churn-response cycle into one that takes minutes. Last quarter, a mid-size SaaS company lost 12% of its at-risk accounts because the retention team took five days to identify and contact dissatisfied customers. By the time someone manually reviewed CSAT spreadsheets and call transcripts, those customers had already churned. Amazon Quick shortens that response window from days to minutes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This post walks through building an automated retention pipeline in Amazon Quick. The pipeline detects dissatisfied customers from structured data. It analyzes sentiment from call transcripts and scores customers by retention priority. It then generates retention offers tailored to each situation. In this post, you learn how to:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Configure a Quick Space with contact center datasets and call transcripts.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Create and register a custom MCP Action for customer scoring.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Build a Chat Agent that combines quantitative key performance indicators (KPIs) with qualitative transcript analysis.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Convert the analysis into a reusable Quick Flow.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Orchestrate the full pipeline with Amazon Quick Automate.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The retention pipeline connects four Amazon Quick components in sequence. Quick Dashboard monitors contact center KPIs, including CSAT (Customer Satisfaction Score, a 1–5 rating customers give after each call), FCR (First Call Resolution, whether the team resolved the issue in a single call), and AHT (Average Handle Time). It identifies customers with CSAT scores at or below 2.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Quick Chat Agent uses natural language to query structured data and unstructured call transcripts. It combines quantitative scores with qualitative sentiment signals. This surfaces why a customer is at risk, not just that they are.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Quick Flows turns the repeatable Chat-based analysis into a scheduled or on-demand automation that runs without manual intervention. Its final step formats the results as a structured list of at-risk customers that downstream automation can consume directly.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Quick Automate executes a multi-step pipeline. It ingests the structured at-risk customer list that the Flow produces and scores those customers through a custom MCP Action. An MCP Action is a serverless endpoint that extends Quick Automate with custom business logic. Refer to the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/mcp-integration.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Quick Automate MCP developer guide&lt;/a&gt; for details. The pipeline then ranks customers by priority, generates retention letters, and uploads them to Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3) for distribution.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You run the workflow on AWS infrastructure and can access audit trails, breakpoints, and role-based access controls.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="automate-pipeline-architecture"&gt;Automate pipeline architecture&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The pipeline executes five sequential steps. It downloads the list of at-risk customers and contact center data from Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3). A scoring step ranks those customers by retention priority (based on CSAT and how recently each customer had an issue) and picks the top two highest-priority cases. The pipeline then drafts bonus-credit letters that reference each customer’s specific issues, saves them as a PDF, and uploads the finished letters to Amazon S3 for delivery and archival.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following table summarizes each step:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Step&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Action&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Details&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Download at-risk customers and contact center dataset&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Download the at-risk customers file (Negative Sentiment.doc) as well as the structured dataset contact_center_data.csv from Amazon S3.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;2&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Retention scoring&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Score each customer on CSAT and issue recency through the custom MCP Action, and pick the top two highest-priority cases.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;3&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Retention letter generation&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Draft bonus-credit letters that reference each customer’s specific issues.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;4&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;PDF creation&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Save the generated letters as a PDF document.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;5&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Upload to Amazon S3&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Store the generated letters in Amazon S3 for delivery and archival.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This solution uses Amazon S3, Amazon Quick (Dashboard, Chat, Flows, Automate), and custom MCP Actions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To follow this walkthrough, you need:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An AWS account with Amazon Quick activated (Dashboard, Chat, Flows, and Automate)&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A structured contact center dataset in CSV format named contact_center_data.csv (columns: customer_id, csat_score, call_date, call_reason, team_id). A sample dataset is provided with this post.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Call transcript documents named Call_transcripts.docx in PDF or TXT format.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An Amazon S3 bucket for pipeline input and output (example: amzn-s3-demo-bucket)&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-walkthrough"&gt;Solution walkthrough&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following sections walk through the build end to end, from preparing your Quick Space and registering the MCP Action connector to assembling the Chat Agent, Flow, and Automate workflow. Complete them in order, because each step builds on the components created before it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="to-prepare-your-quick-space"&gt;To prepare your Quick Space&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A Quick Space is the data layer that gives the Chat Agent access to both structured datasets (for filtering and aggregation) and unstructured documents (for sentiment analysis). Creating the Space first helps verify that every downstream component draws from the same source of truth.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;From the Quick console, select Spaces.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-1-1.png" alt="Amazon Quick console with Spaces selected" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol start="2" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Select “Create space”.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;For &lt;strong&gt;Space name&lt;/strong&gt;, enter ContactSpace.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-2-1.png" alt="Create space dialog with the space name set to ContactSpace" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol start="4" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In the screen that follows, select &lt;strong&gt;Datasets&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/image3b.png"&gt;&lt;img loading="lazy" class="alignnone wp-image-135989 size-full" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/image3b.png" alt="Space configuration screen with the Upload files button highlighted for selection" width="2000" height="1121"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol start="5" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Then select &lt;strong&gt;Add datasets&lt;/strong&gt;. Search for and select the checkbox next to your contact_center_data dataset, then choose Add.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-4-1.png" alt="Add datasets panel with the contact_center_data dataset selected" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol start="6" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Select File Uploads, then select Upload Files, and upload your Call_transcripts.docx transcript file, then choose Open.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-3.png" alt="Space configuration screen with the File Uploads option for the transcript file" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol start="7" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Wait for indexing to complete. The Status column shows &lt;strong&gt;Ready&lt;/strong&gt; for both the dataset and the document.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-6-1.png" alt="Space data sources showing Ready status for the dataset and document" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="to-create-the-mcp-action-connector"&gt;To create the MCP Action connector&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;An MCP Action connector bridges Amazon Quick and your custom business logic. It defines the connector and makes it available as an HTTP endpoint. You write an AWS Lambda function that contains your scoring algorithm wrapped in the MCP protocol, make it available through Amazon API Gateway, and register that endpoint in Amazon Quick.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard that lets AI assistants such as Amazon Quick call external tools in a consistent request-and-response format. If you are new to these services, AWS Lambda lets you run code without provisioning servers, and Amazon API Gateway exposes that code as a web address (an HTTP endpoint) that Amazon Quick can call. You first create the Lambda function, then put an API Gateway endpoint in front of it, and finally register that endpoint in Amazon Quick.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following Lambda function implements a minimal MCP server. It handles the three JSON-RPC methods Amazon Quick calls during connection: initialize (the handshake), tools/list (which advertises the score_customers tool so Amazon Quick can discover it), and tools/call (which runs the scoring when the tool is invoked). The tool’s inputSchema follows JSON Schema Draft 7, which Amazon Quick requires at publish time:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;import json

# Tool catalog advertised to Amazon Quick during discovery (tools/list).
# inputSchema uses JSON Schema Draft 7 (required is an array at the root).
TOOLS = [
    {
        "name": "score_customers",
        "description": "Scores contact center customers by retention priority.",
        "inputSchema": {
            "type": "object",
            "properties": {
                "customers": {
                    "type": "array",
                    "description": "Customer records to score.",
                    "items": {
                        "type": "object",
                        "properties": {
                            "customer_id": {"type": "string"},
                            "csat_score": {"type": "number"},
                            "days_since_last_issue": {"type": "number"}
                        },
                        "required": ["customer_id"]
                    }
                }
            },
            "required": ["customers"]
        }
    }
]


def score_customers(customers):
    scored = []
    for c in customers:
        score = 0
        # Weight factors: low CSAT = high churn risk
        score += (5 - c.get("csat_score", 3)) * 20
        # Recency: issues in last 7 days score higher
        score += 30 if c.get("days_since_last_issue", 30) &amp;lt; 7 else 0
        scored.append({
            "customer_id": c["customer_id"],
            "retention_score": round(score, 1),
            "explanation": f"CSAT:{c.get('csat_score')}, days_since_last_issue:{c.get('days_since_last_issue')}"
        })
    scored.sort(key=lambda x: x["retention_score"], reverse=True)
    return scored


def lambda_handler(event, context):
    """MCP-compliant JSON-RPC handler for customer retention scoring."""
    req = json.loads(event.get("body", "{}"))
    method = req.get("method")
    req_id = req.get("id")

    def rpc(result):
        return {"statusCode": 200,
                "headers": {"Content-Type": "application/json"},
                "body": json.dumps({"jsonrpc": "2.0", "id": req_id, "result": result})}

    if method == "initialize":
        return rpc({
            "protocolVersion": "2024-11-05",
            "capabilities": {"tools": {}},
            "serverInfo": {"name": "customer-scoring", "version": "1.0.0"}
        })

    if method == "tools/list":
        return rpc({"tools": TOOLS})

    if method == "tools/call":
        params = req.get("params", {})
        if params.get("name") == "score_customers":
            args = params.get("arguments", {})
            scored = score_customers(args.get("customers", []))
            return rpc({"content": [{"type": "text", "text": json.dumps(scored)}]})

    return {"statusCode": 200,
            "headers": {"Content-Type": "application/json"},
            "body": json.dumps({"jsonrpc": "2.0", "id": req_id,
                                "error": {"code": -32601, "message": "Method not found"}})}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Deploy this function to AWS Lambda:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol start="8" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Open the AWS Lambda console and choose Create function. Select Author from scratch.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;For Function name, enter customer-scoring. For Runtime, choose a recent supported Python version (for example, Python 3.13). Choose Create function.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In the Code tab, replace the default code with the function shown earlier, then choose Deploy.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With the function deployed, create an API Gateway endpoint in front of it using the following AWS Command Line Interface (AWS CLI) commands. If you prefer, you can perform these same steps in the API Gateway console. Run the commands in order. Each one produces a value (such as the API ID) that you use in the next.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Replace the placeholder values (, , , ) with your own:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;# 1. Create REST API
aws apigateway create-rest-api
--name customer-scoring-api
--endpoint-configuration types=REGIONAL

# Note the "id" value from the response -&amp;gt; &amp;lt;API_ID&amp;gt;

# 2. Get root resource ID
aws apigateway get-resources --rest-api-id &amp;lt;API_ID&amp;gt;

# Note the "id" value -&amp;gt; &amp;lt;ROOT_RESOURCE_ID&amp;gt;

# 3. Create POST method
aws apigateway put-method
--rest-api-id &amp;lt;API_ID&amp;gt;
--resource-id &amp;lt;ROOT_RESOURCE_ID&amp;gt;
--http-method POST
--authorization-type NONE

# 4. Integrate with Lambda
aws apigateway put-integration
--rest-api-id &amp;lt;API_ID&amp;gt;
--resource-id &amp;lt;ROOT_RESOURCE_ID&amp;gt;
--http-method POST
--type AWS_PROXY
--integration-http-method POST
--uri "arn:aws:apigateway:&amp;lt;REGION&amp;gt;:lambda:path/2015-03-31/functions/arn:aws:lambda:&amp;lt;REGION&amp;gt;:&amp;lt;ACCOUNT_ID&amp;gt;:function:&amp;lt;LAMBDA_FUNCTION_NAME&amp;gt;/invocations"

# 5. Allow API Gateway to invoke Lambda
aws lambda add-permission
--function-name &amp;lt;LAMBDA_FUNCTION_NAME&amp;gt;
--statement-id apigateway-invoke
--action lambda:InvokeFunction
--principal apigateway.amazonaws.com
--source-arn "arn:aws:execute-api:&amp;lt;REGION&amp;gt;:&amp;lt;ACCOUNT_ID&amp;gt;:&amp;lt;API_ID&amp;gt;/*/POST/"

# 6. Deploy
aws apigateway create-deployment
--rest-api-id &amp;lt;API_ID&amp;gt;
--stage-name prod

# Your endpoint URL:

# https://&amp;lt;API_ID&amp;gt;.execute-api.&amp;lt;REGION&amp;gt;.amazonaws.com/prod/mcp&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The create-deployment command returns a deployment id and timestamp. Construct the endpoint URL yourself using your API ID and Region in the following format: &lt;code&gt;https://&amp;lt;API_ID&amp;gt;.execute-api.&amp;lt;REGION&amp;gt;.amazonaws.com/prod/mcp&lt;/code&gt;. Note this URL. You use it when registering the connector in Amazon Quick.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Production consideration: This walkthrough uses API Gateway directly in front of Lambda for simplicity. For production workloads, consider using Agentcore gateways in front of Lambda, which provide built-in, agent-level authentication, throttling, and observability out of the box.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h4 id="to-register-the-mcp-action-connector-in-amazon-quick"&gt;To register the MCP Action connector in Amazon Quick&lt;/h4&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;From the Amazon Quick home page, select &lt;strong&gt;More&lt;/strong&gt;, then select &lt;strong&gt;Connectors&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In the right section, select Create for your team.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-7-1.png" alt="Connectors page with the Create for your team option" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt; 
&lt;ol start="3" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Select &lt;strong&gt;Model Context Protocol&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-8-1.png" alt="Connector type selection showing Model Context Protocol" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt; 
&lt;ol start="4" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In the screen that follows, Select &lt;strong&gt;No, create new&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-9-1.png" alt="New connector prompt with No, create new selected" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt; 
&lt;ol start="5" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Enter the following values and choose &lt;strong&gt;Next&lt;/strong&gt;:&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Name&lt;/strong&gt;: mcp-customer-score.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Description&lt;/strong&gt;: Find score of customers based on aggregated KPIs.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;MCP server endpoint&lt;/strong&gt;: paste the endpoint URL &lt;code&gt;https://&amp;lt;API_ID&amp;gt;.execute-api.&amp;lt;REGION&amp;gt;.amazonaws.com/prod/mcp&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Connection type&lt;/strong&gt;: Public network.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-10-1.png" alt="MCP connector form with name, description, endpoint, and public network connection type" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol start="6" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Next&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;On the &lt;strong&gt;Authenticate&lt;/strong&gt; step, select &lt;strong&gt;Service authentication&lt;/strong&gt;, then open the &lt;strong&gt;Auth configuration&lt;/strong&gt; list and choose &lt;strong&gt;None&lt;/strong&gt;. This endpoint is intentionally open for demonstration purposes. In a production environment, you would typically add an authentication layer, most commonly two-legged OAuth (2LO), to secure the endpoint. Because the scoring endpoint you deployed uses –authorization-type NONE, it accepts unauthenticated requests, so no client ID or secret is required. (Use an OAuth option only if your MCP server is protected by OAuth.)&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-11.png" alt="Authenticate step with Service authentication and Auth configuration set to None" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol start="8" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Create and continue&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Publish&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Wait a few minutes, then choose the new connector in the &lt;strong&gt;Available&lt;/strong&gt; section. Confirm the status shows &lt;strong&gt;Ready&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-12-1.png" alt="Available connectors list showing the mcp-customer-score connector with Ready status" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="to-create-a-custom-chat-agent-for-contact-center-analysis"&gt;To create a custom Chat Agent for contact center analysis&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A custom Chat Agent connects to your ContactSpace and has access to the MCP Action connector you registered. The system prompt is the critical component. It tells the agent how to interpret the data, what questions to ask of the transcripts, and when to invoke the scoring tool versus answering from the dataset directly.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;From the Quick home page, select Chat agents, then choose Blank, followed by Skip to create a custom chat agent from scratch.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;For &lt;strong&gt;Agent name&lt;/strong&gt;, enter Contact Center Strategy Analyst.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Under &lt;strong&gt;Knowledge Sources&lt;/strong&gt;, link the ContactSpace.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Under &lt;strong&gt;Actions&lt;/strong&gt;, add the mcp-customer-score connector.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;For the system prompt, enter text similar to the following:&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;pre class="text"&gt;&lt;code&gt;You are a Contact Center Strategy Analyst. You have access to:
1. contact_center_data.csv - structured CSAT, call, and team data
2. Call_transcripts.docx - unstructured call transcript documents
3. mcp-customer-score tool - calculates retention priority scores
When asked about at-risk customers:
- Query the structured dataset for low CSAT scores
- Cross-reference with transcripts to identify sentiment and root causes
- When asked to score customers, call the mcp-customer-score tool
- Always explain WHY a customer is at risk, not just that they are
- Format results as tables when showing multiple customers&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;ol start="6" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Launch chat agent&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-13-1.png" alt="Chat agent configuration with the Launch chat agent option" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="to-analyze-at-risk-customers-with-the-chat-agent"&gt;To analyze at-risk customers with the Chat Agent&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Chat Agent accesses both the structured dataset and unstructured transcripts in your Space. Use it to identify at-risk customers and understand the reasons behind their low satisfaction scores.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;From the Amazon Quick home page, chat with the new custom chat agent. First, choose the &lt;strong&gt;Contact Center Strategy Analyst&lt;/strong&gt; agent from the list.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Enter the following prompt:&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;pre class="text"&gt;&lt;code&gt;Show me customers that rated low CSAT (at or below 2) using contact center data&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent returns a table of customer IDs, call dates, CSAT scores, and related metrics. This identifies who is at risk but not why.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol start="3" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Enter a follow-up prompt:&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;pre class="text"&gt;&lt;code&gt;Using the call transcripts, summarize the main reasons these customers were dissatisfied&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent reads the call transcripts and summarizes its findings. For example, it might identify that Customer 4471 expressed repeated frustration about billing errors across three calls. Customer 8832 had a single intense interaction about a service outage. This distinction matters. Repeated small frustrations require a different retention approach than one major incident.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol start="4" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;To produce a structured result that downstream automation can consume, enter a final prompt:&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;pre class="text"&gt;&lt;code&gt;For the top 20 most at-risk customers, return only a JSON array where
each object has customer_id, csat_score, and days_since_last_issue
(calculated from the most recent call date). Do not include any other text.&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent returns a structured JSON list of the at-risk customers. Copy this JSON output from the chat response and save it as a file named at_risk_customers.json, then upload it to your Amazon S3 bucket (for example, to the inputs/ prefix). The automation downloads this file from Amazon S3 in a later step.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-14-1.png" alt="Chat agent returning a JSON array of at-risk customers" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="to-convert-the-analysis-into-a-quick-flow"&gt;To convert the analysis into a Quick Flow&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Beyond the one-time analysis, Quick Flows lets you turn a Chat conversation into a reusable automation with a single step, so the same analysis can be re-run on demand or on a schedule. The following steps demonstrate how to convert the conversation you just had into a Flow.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In the Chat interface, choose the + on the toolbar, then select Flow.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Select Generate new flow, keep the suggested prompt and select Generate.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Review the flow then choose Publish.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Select Run mode, if required enter any inputs then start the flow.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Flow is now a reusable automation. Share it with your team or schedule it to run weekly to regenerate the analysis whenever you need it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-15-1.png" alt="Quick Flow generated from the chat conversation" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="to-create-the-automate-workflow"&gt;To create the Automate workflow&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Connectors required: these connectors should be part of the Automation group that we create the Automate in.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon S3 (two connections: one for source data, one for output uploads)&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Custom Agent (with MCP scoring agent created in the MCP action connector step)&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h4 id="to-create-an-automation-group"&gt;To create an Automation group&lt;/h4&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;On the Amazon Quick home page, choose More, then Automations.&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-16.png" alt="Amazon Quick home page with the Automations option under More" width="800"&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In the page that follows, choose the ‘Groups’ tab.&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-17-1.png" alt="Automations page with the Groups tab selected" width="800"&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Then choose Create Group&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-18-1.png" alt="Groups tab with the Create Group option" width="800"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-19-1.png" alt="Create group dialog" width="800"&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose the required actions, the MCP action and the Amazon S3 action. Then choose Next.&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-20-1.png" alt="Group action selection with the MCP and Amazon S3 actions chosen" width="800"&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;On the following page, choose Done&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-21.png" alt="Group creation confirmation with the Done option" width="800"&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h4 id="to-create-the-automation-project"&gt;To create the Automation project&lt;/h4&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The above group will be used to create the Automation project. Follow the instructions to create the Automation project.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;On the Amazon Quick home page, choose More, then Automations.&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-22-1.png" alt="Amazon Quick home page with the Automations option selected" width="800"&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;On the screen that follows, choose Create Project, then Create Project.&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-23-1.png" alt="Automations page with the Create Project option" width="800"&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Enter a name for the automation, choose the Automation group, then choose Create&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-24-1.png" alt="New automation project dialog with a name and automation group selected" width="800"&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;On the following screen, choose Start building&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-25-1.png" alt="New project screen with the Start building option" width="800"&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose Skip&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-26.png" alt="Automation builder prompt with the Skip option" width="800"&gt;The following screen appears&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-27-1.png" alt="Empty Automate canvas ready for building steps" width="800"&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h4 id="to-build-the-automation-steps"&gt;To build the Automation steps&lt;/h4&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For the subsequent steps, follow the instructions shown below and complete the Automation project.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Step 1: Load call data from Amazon S3&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Choose New step and enter the following inside the chat assistant on the left that says ‘What do you want to automate’. This initial step loads call data from the raw contact center CSV in Amazon S3:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;pre class="text"&gt;&lt;code&gt;Log an info message: "Starting download of customer contact data from S3"
Download the file "contact_center_data_complete.csv" from the S3 bucket contactcenterbucketdemo".
Read the downloaded file as a CSV with headers included and comma delimiter. Store the result as call_data.
Log an info message: "Successfully loaded customer contact data from S3 CSV file"
Have all the above under one step&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-28-1.png" alt="Chat assistant showing the Step 1 instruction to download and read the contact center CSV" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Choose Build Step 1.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-29-1.png" alt="Automate canvas after building Step 1" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Choose the close (x) symbol in the top right.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-30.png" alt="Step configuration panel with the close control in the top right" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The step will be created as shown below.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-31-1.png" alt="Automate canvas showing the created Step 1" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Step 2: Filter high-risk customers&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Choose the add (+) symbol below the step, then choose Process step.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-32.png" alt="Add step menu with Process step selected" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Toggle the mode back to planning. Enter the following in the chat window, then choose the talk symbol.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;pre class="text"&gt;&lt;code&gt;Log an info message: "Starting to filter call data for known high-risk customers with negative sentiment"
Download the file "input/NegativeSentiment.docx" from the S3 bucket "contactcenterbucketdemo".
Have all the above under one step&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/24/ML-21125-33-1.png" alt="Chat window showing the Step 2 instruction to filter high-risk customers" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Step 3: Aggregate customer metrics&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Repeat the above for step 3 (Aggregate Customer Metrics) with following instruction:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;pre class="text"&gt;&lt;code&gt;Loop through each item in the aggregated metrics result table.
For each item, log an info message with this exact format:
"Customer: {item['customer_id']}, Total Calls: {item['total_calls']}, Avg CSAT: {item['avg_csat_score']}, FCR Rate: {item['first_call_resolution_rate']}%, Resolved Rate: {item['resolved_rate']}%, Avg Transfers: {item['avg_transfer_count']}, Avg Hold Time: {item['avg_hold_time_seconds']}s, Complaint Ratio: {item['complaint_ratio']}"
Have all the above under one step&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These two instructions together will recreate the full Step 3 including its nested sub-step for logging. The main step handles all the calculation logic, and the sub-step handles the per-customer logging output.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Step 4: Identify top customers and generate letters&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Repeat the above for step 4 (Identify Top customers and generate letter) with following instruction:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;pre class="text"&gt;&lt;code&gt;Log an info message: "Starting customer bonus letter generation process"
Collect all rows from customer_aggregated_metrics into a list called customer_records by looping through the table and appending each row.
Use an inline agent in "pro" mode with this instruction:
"Analyze the provided customer aggregated metrics data: {customer_records}. This data contains real customer records with actual customer IDs and performance scores. Identify and return the top 2 performing customers based on their aggregated scores from this actual data. Do not generate fictional customer IDs - use only the real customer IDs present in the provided customer records data. Return the actual customer information including the real customer IDs and their corresponding scores from the data."
Set the output schema to an object with a required property "top_customers" which is an array (minContains 2, maxContains 2) of objects. Each object has "customer_id" (string, required) and "score" (number).
Give the agent these tools: python_repl (coding tool) and the configured MCP tools for customer lookup.
Log the result: "top_customers are:" followed by the top_customers value.
Store the top_customers list. Create a helper function called generate_timestamp that returns the current datetime formatted as "%Y%m%d_%H%M%S". Call it and store as timestamp.
Create an empty list called bonus_letters.
For each customer in top_customers:
1. Get the customer_id from the customer object.
2. Create a PDF using create_pdf with the following HTML content:
   - Style: body with font-family Arial, line-height 1.6, margin 40px, color #333
   - A "header" div centered with h2 "Customer Retention Department" and italic paragraph "Valued Customer Appreciation Program"
   - A "content" div containing:
   - Bold greeting: "Dear Valued Customer (ID: {customer_id}),"
   - Paragraph expressing appreciation for continued partnership
   - Paragraph acknowledging past service issues and offering heartfelt apologies, mentioning their feedback has been invaluable
   - A "highlight" div (background-color #f0f8ff, padding 15px, border-left 4px solid #007acc, margin 20px 0) containing bold text: "As a gesture of our commitment to your satisfaction and our appreciation for your loyalty, we are pleased to offer you a $100 bonus credit."
   - Paragraph about the bonus reflecting genuine desire to make things right and mutual success
   - Paragraph about enhanced service protocols and dedicated customer success team
   - Paragraph hoping for opportunity to demonstrate renewed commitment
   - A "signature" div with margin-top 40px containing:
   - "With sincere regards and appreciation,"
   - A line break
   - Bold "Customer Retention Team", then "Customer Success Department", then italic "Dedicated to Your Success"
3. Upload the PDF to S3 bucket "contactcenterbucketdemo" with Key = "output/" + customer_id + timestamp + ".pdf"
4. Append the PDF to the bonus_letters list.
After the loop completes, log: "Successfully generated bonus letters for top 2 customers"
Have all the above under one step&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="to-test-and-deploy-the-automation"&gt;To test and deploy the automation&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Validate the automation end to end, then commit and deploy it so it can run on a schedule.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In the canvas, choose &lt;strong&gt;Debug&lt;/strong&gt; (or &lt;strong&gt;Test&lt;/strong&gt;) to run the automation. It runs on its own, downloading the at-risk customers file from Amazon S3 through the Download file step. Review each step’s output in the logs panel.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Confirm the scoring step returns the top two customers, the letters reference the correct customer issues, and the S3 upload completes successfully.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;When the run is correct, choose &lt;strong&gt;Commit&lt;/strong&gt; to create a version.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Deploy&lt;/strong&gt;, select the committed version, and confirm the required credentials and connections (your Amazon S3 connection and the mcp-customer-score connector).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;To run the automation on a schedule, on the &lt;strong&gt;Deployment&lt;/strong&gt; page choose &lt;strong&gt;Create Trigger&lt;/strong&gt;, then set the frequency (or a cron expression), start time, and time zone.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For more details refer to the following links.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a class="uri" href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/testing-automations.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/testing-automations.html&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a class="uri" href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/deploying-automations.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/deploying-automations.html&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="results"&gt;Results&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;After deploying this pipeline in a test environment with historical contact center data, the team measured improvements across four dimensions: response time, retention rates, offer acceptance, and time to deploy. Internal testing with sample data produced these results. Your implementation may vary.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Response time&lt;/strong&gt; dropped from days of manual identification and drafting to minutes of automated execution.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Retention rates&lt;/strong&gt; improved among flagged customers compared to the previous reactive approach. Faster outreach, while the experience is still fresh, drove this improvement.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Offer acceptance&lt;/strong&gt; increased when letters referenced the customer’s specific issue rather than offering a generic discount.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Time to deploy&lt;/strong&gt; took less than one day. Business users built the full workflow through natural language and point-and-click screens.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="troubleshooting"&gt;Troubleshooting&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following table lists common issues and their resolutions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Symptom&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cause&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Resolution&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Chat Agent returns “No data found”&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Space indexing incomplete&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Verify ContactSpace status. Wait for the Ready indicator before running queries.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Sentiment analysis is generic&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Transcripts not indexed&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Verify call transcript documents are uploaded to the Space Documents section.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Automation scores no customers or the wrong ones&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;At-risk JSON not found, or the agent did not receive it&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Confirm at_risk_customers.json exists at the Amazon S3 path the Download file step references, and that the scoring agent’s instructions reference the downloaded variable.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;MCP Action returns timeout&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Endpoint not responding&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Increase the MCP Action timeout or reduce the dataset scope.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Letters are generic or missing customer details&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Letter agent did not receive the scored results&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Confirm the letter agent’s prompt references the scoring step’s output variable (inserted with @).&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="cleaning-up"&gt;Cleaning up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To avoid ongoing costs when you no longer need these resources:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Delete the Automate workflow.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Delete the Flow.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Remove input datasets and generated letters from your S3 bucket:&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws s3 rm s3://amzn-s3-demo-bucket/inputs/ --recursive
aws s3 rm s3://amzn-s3-demo-bucket/output/letters/ --recursive&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;ol start="4" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Deregister the custom MCP Action endpoint if it was created specifically for this workflow.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Remove uploaded data from ContactSpace if no longer needed.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This walkthrough demonstrated how to build a customer retention pipeline that goes from raw contact center data to targeted retention actions, without writing application code. The pipeline combines KPI monitoring through Quick Dashboard and AI-powered transcript analysis through the Chat Agent. It adds repeatable automation through Quick Flows and multi-step processing through Quick Automate.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;After the initial developer setup (Lambda function and API Gateway), business users built and operated the remaining components through natural language and point-and-click interfaces. The MCP Action connector extended the pipeline with custom scoring logic that reflects your organization’s specific retention criteria.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Apply this same pattern to adjacent use cases: client account health monitoring, compliance risk escalation, or proactive service recovery. Start by connecting your data to a Quick Space and asking questions through the Chat Agent. Once you have the right analysis, convert it to a Flow and orchestrate the workflow with Automate.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For more information, refer to the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/what-is.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick documentation&lt;/a&gt;. To explore the MCP Action framework, refer to the &lt;span&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/mcp-integration.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"&gt;MCP action framework&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/span&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>How AgentCore Gateway supports the MCP 2026-07-28 spec</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/how-agentcore-gateway-supports-the-mcp-2026-07-28-spec/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Sean Eichenberger]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Tue, 28 Jul 2026 19:07:09 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Intermediate (200)]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">6f308dab489ca3e002058919cc2813576498c4ee</guid>

					<description>The Model Context Protocol (MCP) published its 2026-07-28 specification, the largest revision since launch: MCP is now stateless, with a governed extensions system and hardened authorization. Learn what changed and how to enable the new version on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Gateway with a single UpdateGateway call.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Today, the Model Context Protocol (MCP) published its 2026-07-28 specification, the largest and most significant revision of the protocol since its launch. With this release MCP becomes a stateless protocol that scales on ordinary HTTP infrastructure. Alongside the transport changes, this new version introduces a governed extensions system, strengthens authorization by aligning more closely with enterprise practices for OAuth 2.0 and OpenID Connect, and establishes lifecycle guarantees that limit future breakage.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can start using the latest protocol on your &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/gateway.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Gateway&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, today by calling &lt;code&gt;UpdateGateway&lt;/code&gt; with the list of versions you want your gateway to support. Existing clients keep working exactly as before, and there is no per-target step. In this post, we walk through what changed in the protocol and why, what it means for your agents and tools, and how to enable the new version on your gateways.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Already familiar with the spec changes? Jump straight to &lt;a href="#updating-your-agentcore-gateway"&gt;Updating your AgentCore Gateway&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="what-changes-with-the-new-spec-and-why-it-matters"&gt;What changes with the new spec and why it matters&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This release contains &lt;strong&gt;backward-incompatible changes&lt;/strong&gt; to the MCP protocol. The transition to statelessness is a foundational change that protocol maintainers believe is necessary to address scaling challenges with enterprise deployments. The introduction of breaking changes with new versions, however, is not expected to be the norm. To help facilitate backward-compatible updates in future revisions, maintainers have introduced new governance enhancements to the protocol specification (a feature lifecycle policy, an extensions framework, and a conformance-suite requirement). These enhancements are designed to support protocol evolution without breaking core capabilities.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Upgrading is also &lt;strong&gt;opt-in&lt;/strong&gt;, and nothing changes until both you and your clients act. AgentCore Gateway advertises the protocol versions it speaks through a single configuration field. Clients select a version on every request. Adding 2026-07-28 to a gateway that also advertises 2025-11-25 does not change anything for clients that request the older version. Only a client that requests 2026-07-28 gets the new behavior. Nothing breaks on July 28.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="mcp-is-now-stateless"&gt;1. MCP is now stateless&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In prior versions of the protocol, every Streamable HTTP interaction with an MCP server began with an &lt;code&gt;initialize&lt;/code&gt;/&lt;code&gt;initialized&lt;/code&gt; handshake, in which the client and server exchanged protocol versions and capabilities. The server then issued an &lt;code&gt;Mcp-Session-Id&lt;/code&gt; header that every subsequent request was required to carry:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;pre class="http"&gt;&lt;code&gt;POST /mcp HTTP/1.1
Mcp-Session-Id: 1868a90c-3a3f-4f5b
Content-Type: application/json
Accept: application/json

{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":2,"method":"tools/call",
"params":{"name":"search","arguments":{"q":"otters"}}}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;That session pinned the client to the server that issued it. Therefore, to scale an MCP server horizontally, operators needed sticky sessions at the load balancer, a shared session store behind the fleet, or both.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;By removing sessions, remote MCP servers effectively become standard HTTPS endpoints, which scale cleanly with enterprise workloads. As the protocol becomes stateless, the handshake and the protocol-level session are both no longer needed (&lt;a class="Hyperlink TrackedChange TrackChangeHyperlinkInstruction SCXW147330082 BCX0" href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2575-stateless-mcp" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun Underlined SCXW147330082 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="none"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW147330082 BCX0"&gt;SEP-2575&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW147330082 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW147330082 BCX0"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW147330082 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW147330082 BCX0"&gt;and&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;a class="Hyperlink TrackedChange TrackChangeHyperlinkInstruction SCXW147330082 BCX0" href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2567-sessionless-mcp" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun Underlined SCXW147330082 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="none"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW147330082 BCX0"&gt;SEP-2567&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW147330082 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW147330082 BCX0"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;remove these requirements from the new protocol version). With 2026-07-28, every request now carries protocol version, client info, and client capabilities inside its &lt;code&gt;_meta&lt;/code&gt; parameter, eliminating the need for a one-time initialization handshake. Clients that need to learn what a server supports can call the new &lt;code&gt;server/discover&lt;/code&gt; method at any point. The net effect is that a single tool call is fully self-contained. It requires no prior session context and can be routed to any server instance. Removing the protocol session does not preclude support for stateful applications. When servers need continuity across calls, you can follow established patterns for HTTP requests by passing an explicit ID as a tool parameter, which should come from one of your own tools.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;pre class="http"&gt;&lt;code&gt;POST /mcp HTTP/1.1
MCP-Protocol-Version: 2026-07-28
Mcp-Method: tools/call
Mcp-Name: create_basket
Content-Type: application/json
Accept: application/json,text/event-stream

{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"tools/call",
"params":{"name":"create_basket","arguments":{},
"_meta":{"io.modelcontextprotocol/clientInfo":{"name":"my-app","version":"1.0"}}}}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Response:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,
"result":{"resultType":"complete","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Created basket bsk_a1b2c3"}],"structuredContent":{"basket_id":"bsk_a1b2c3"},"isError": false}}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can then use the agent’s own capabilities to thread this state handle through subsequent requests:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;pre class="http"&gt;&lt;code&gt;POST /mcp HTTP/1.1
MCP-Protocol-Version: 2026-07-28
Mcp-Method: tools/call
Mcp-Name: add_item
Content-Type: application/json
Accept: application/json,text/event-stream

{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"tools/call",
"params":{"name":"add_item","arguments":{"basket_id": "bsk_a1b2c3", "sku": "shoes"},
"_meta":{"io.modelcontextprotocol/clientInfo":{"name":"my-app","version":"1.0"}}}}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AgentCore Gateway has always shielded agents from much of MCP’s session mechanics. It aggregates your AWS Lambda functions, APIs, and MCP servers behind a single MCP endpoint and manages the protocol conversation with each target on your behalf. With 2026-07-28, that gets simpler on both sides: an agent’s first tool call is one self-contained request rather than a handshake followed by a call. Each request carries an &lt;code&gt;Mcp-Protocol-Version&lt;/code&gt; header. The gateway serves the request in that version when the version appears in &lt;code&gt;supportedVersions&lt;/code&gt;, or rejects it with &lt;code&gt;HTTP 400&lt;/code&gt; (code &lt;code&gt;-32022&lt;/code&gt;) and a list of supported versions when it does not. Requests that omit the header default to 2025-03-26.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="routable-cacheable-traceable-traffic"&gt;2. Routing,&amp;nbsp;caching, and&amp;nbsp;tracing&amp;nbsp;without&amp;nbsp;parsing the&amp;nbsp;body&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In prior versions of the protocol, the operation a request performed was opaque to anything sitting between client and server. Load balancers, API gateways, and rate limiters all had to parse the JSON-RPC body to determine what method was being called. Detecting changes to a server’s tool catalog meant holding a long-lived SSE stream open and waiting for a push notification. MCP traffic, in short, did not play well with standard HTTP infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;2026-07-28 closes that gap in three ways. First, every Streamable HTTP request now surfaces its intent in standard headers: &lt;code&gt;Mcp-Method&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;Mcp-Name&lt;/code&gt; (&lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2243-http-standardization" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SEP-2243&lt;/a&gt;) travel outside the body, giving intermediaries enough information to route, throttle, and meter at the HTTP layer alone. A server that receives a request where the declared headers contradict the body rejects it outright. Second, responses to list and resource-read operations now include explicit freshness metadata, &lt;code&gt;ttlMs&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;cacheScope&lt;/code&gt;, borrowed from the semantics of HTTP &lt;code&gt;Cache-Control&lt;/code&gt; (&lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2549-TTL-for-list-results" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SEP-2549&lt;/a&gt;). Clients can cache a &lt;code&gt;tools/list&lt;/code&gt; response for a known duration and scope without maintaining a persistent connection only to watch for invalidations. Third, the spec now reserves the W3C Trace Context keys (&lt;code&gt;traceparent&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;tracestate&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;baggage&lt;/code&gt;) inside &lt;code&gt;_meta&lt;/code&gt; (&lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/414-request-meta" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SEP-414&lt;/a&gt;), formalizing what several SDKs had already adopted in practice. A distributed trace originating in your application can now propagate through the full call chain, from agent to MCP client to gateway to downstream service, and render as a unified span tree in any OpenTelemetry-compatible collector.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;On the 2026-07-28 path, AgentCore Gateway surfaces these primitives directly. Every tool result from your gateway carries the new structured result envelope, and cacheable results include TTL and scope hints for conformant client SDKs. The gateway also enforces the custom-header binding: when a tool’s input schema marks a field as header-bound, the gateway rejects any request whose header is absent or contradicts the body (&lt;code&gt;HTTP 400&lt;/code&gt;, code &lt;code&gt;-32020&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="server-to-client-interactions-without-persistent-connections"&gt;3. Server-to-client&amp;nbsp;interactions without&amp;nbsp;persistent&amp;nbsp;connections&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A stateless protocol still needs a way for a server to talk back to the client mid-call. MCP has three such interactions: elicitation, where the server asks the end user for input (“this tool is about to delete three files. Confirm?”), roots, where the server asks for available directories and files in the filesystem, and sampling, where the server asks the client’s model to generate a response. In previous versions, delivering these requests meant the server held an SSE stream open back to the client.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With the latest protocol version, server-initiated requests are now only permitted while the server is actively processing a client request (&lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2260-Require-Server-requests-to-be-associated-with-Client-requests" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SEP-2260&lt;/a&gt;). Every prompt a user sees traces back to something they or their agent started. Multi Round-Trip Requests (&lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2322-MRTR" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SEP-2322&lt;/a&gt;) replace the previous long-lived stream. Here, the server embeds questions in an &lt;code&gt;InputRequiredResult&lt;/code&gt; and externalizes state through an opaque &lt;code&gt;requestState&lt;/code&gt; token rather than pushing a question over SSE. Only tools that declare elicitation or sampling inputs trigger the multi-round-trip exchange, and only when the client advertises support for it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
  "resultType": "input_required",
  "inputRequests": {
    "confirm": {
      "method": "elicitation/create",
      "params": {
        "mode": "form",
        "message": "Delete 3 files?",
        "requestedSchema": {
          "type": "object",
          "properties": {
            "confirm": {
              "type": "boolean"
            },
            "required": ["confirm"]
          }
        }
      }
    },
    "requestState": "eyJzdGVwIjoxLCJmaWxlcyI6WyJhIiwiYiIsImMiXX0="
  }
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Clients collect the user’s answers and re-issue the original call with the responses and the &lt;code&gt;requestState&lt;/code&gt;. Everything the server needs is in the payload, so any server instance can pick up the retry.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Separately, progress notifications and log delivery are now request-scoped. The gateway relays progress updates only when the client supplies a progress token on the originating request, and delivers log messages only when the client sets &lt;code&gt;io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel&lt;/code&gt; in per-request metadata.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="extensions-become-first-class"&gt;4. Decoupling extensions from the core spec&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Until now, MCP extensions were an informal concept with no governance behind them. &lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2133-extensions" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SEP-2133&lt;/a&gt; introduces one. Each extension carries a reverse-DNS ID, is negotiated through an &lt;code&gt;extensions&lt;/code&gt; map in client and server capabilities, lives in a dedicated repository under delegated maintainers, and follows its own release cadence independent of the core spec. A new Extensions Track within the SEP process defines how an extension graduates from experimental to official status, which changes how the protocol grows. Rather than every new idea competing for space in the core specification and forcing a breaking version bump, capabilities can now mature as opt-in extensions on their own timelines. Clients and servers that do not negotiate a given extension are unaffected.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="authorization-hardening"&gt;5. Authorization hardening&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Six SEPs bring MCP’s&amp;nbsp;authorization specification into closer alignment with&amp;nbsp;production OAuth 2.0 and OpenID Connect deployments. Your gateway’s inbound authorization, whether IAM (SigV4) or OAuth/JWT, is unchanged by the protocol version. So are your outbound credential&amp;nbsp;providers. Upgrading the protocol version does not require changing credentials or authorizer configuration.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="error-handling-and-deprecations"&gt;6. Error handling and deprecations&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;On earlier protocol versions, nearly everything came back as HTTP 200 with an error tucked inside the JSON-RPC body, including transport-level failures. On 2026-07-28, the transport and application layers are cleanly separated. Transport failures return real HTTP status codes. Application-level outcomes stay in the body:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Situation&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;2025-* versions&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;2026-07-28&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Unknown method&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;In-body JSON-RPC error, &lt;code&gt;HTTP 200&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;HTTP 404&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Unsupported protocol version&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Varies&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;HTTP 400&lt;/code&gt;, code &lt;code&gt;-32022&lt;/code&gt;. Body lists supported versions&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Header-bound field absent or mismatched&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Varies&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;HTTP 400&lt;/code&gt;, code &lt;code&gt;-32020&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Missing required client capability&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Not applicable&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Code &lt;code&gt;-32021&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These changes only matter for code that inspects error responses. They let your HTTP-level monitoring, alarming, and retry logic distinguish routing problems from application-level issues without parsing the body. The spec also reassigns the “&lt;em&gt;resource not found&lt;/em&gt;” error code from the MCP-specific &lt;code&gt;-32002&lt;/code&gt; to JSON-RPC’s standard &lt;code&gt;-32602&lt;/code&gt; Invalid Params (&lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2164-resource-not-found-error" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SEP-2164&lt;/a&gt;). If your client matches on &lt;code&gt;-32002&lt;/code&gt; anywhere, audit those code paths. Additionally, on the 2026-07-28 path: &lt;code&gt;logging/setLevel&lt;/code&gt; is retired, and a client that calls it is rejected. To opt into log delivery, you can set the per-request metadata field &lt;code&gt;io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel&lt;/code&gt; instead.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Three core features are deprecated under the protocol’s new feature lifecycle policy (&lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2577-deprecate-roots-sampling-and-logging" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SEP-2577&lt;/a&gt;): Roots (replaced by tool parameters, resource URIs, or server configuration), Sampling (replaced by direct integration with LLM provider APIs), and Logging (replaced by &lt;code&gt;stderr&lt;/code&gt; for stdio transports and OpenTelemetry for structured observability). These deprecations are advisory. The methods, types, and capability flags remain functional in this release and in any specification version published within twelve months of it. However, we highly recommend that new implementations not take a dependency on them.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="schema-support"&gt;7. Schema support&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Tool &lt;code&gt;inputSchema&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;outputSchema&lt;/code&gt; now support the full &lt;a href="https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;JSON Schema 2020-12&lt;/a&gt; vocabulary (&lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2106-json-schema-2020-12" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SEP-2106&lt;/a&gt;). Input schemas retain the &lt;code&gt;type: "object"&lt;/code&gt; root requirement but gain composition keywords (&lt;code&gt;oneOf&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;anyOf&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;allOf&lt;/code&gt;), conditionals, and internal references (&lt;code&gt;$ref&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;$defs&lt;/code&gt;). Output schemas have no root-type restriction, and &lt;code&gt;structuredContent&lt;/code&gt; may be any valid JSON value. Implementations must not follow external &lt;code&gt;$ref&lt;/code&gt; URIs automatically and should enforce depth and time limits during validation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="deciding-if-youre-ready-to-update"&gt;Deciding if you’re ready to update&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Work through three questions before you enable the new version:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Are your clients ready?&lt;/strong&gt; Your agents reach Gateway through MCP client SDKs. Check that the SDKs your agent frameworks and host applications use have shipped&amp;nbsp;2026-07-28&amp;nbsp;support. Tier 1 SDKs were expected to ship support during the ten-week release candidate window, so most mainstream stacks are ready today. See the&amp;nbsp;&lt;a class="Hyperlink SCXW148261944 BCX0" href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/sdk" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"&gt;MCP SDK documentation&lt;/a&gt;&amp;nbsp;for the current tier list. Test clients that support&amp;nbsp;2026-07-28 before you advertise it on a production gateway.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Does anything you run depend on removed or changed behavior?&lt;/strong&gt; Audit for: protocol sessions used to carry application state, &lt;code&gt;logging/setLevel&lt;/code&gt;, client code matching the literal &lt;code&gt;-32002&lt;/code&gt; error code, and any dependence on Roots, Sampling, or Logging. If your tools use elicitation or sampling, note that the gateway carries these through a new per-request mechanism on 2026-07-28 rather than a persistent session. Confirm your clients declare the matching capability. A client that omits it is rejected with code &lt;code&gt;-32021&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Do you control both sides?&lt;/strong&gt; You don’t need to. Version selection happens per request, so a dual-version gateway serves old and new clients simultaneously. You can upgrade clients and the gateway on independent schedules, in either order.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For the full details, see the&amp;nbsp;MCP 2026-07-28 specification the &lt;a class="Hyperlink TrackedChange SCXW247456459 BCX0" href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/changelog" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"&gt;changelog against 2025-11-25&lt;/a&gt;, and the&amp;nbsp;&lt;a class="Hyperlink SCXW247456459 BCX0" href="https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/2026-07-28-release-candidate/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"&gt;MCP blog’s release candidate announcement&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="updating-your-agentcore-gateway"&gt;Updating your AgentCore Gateway&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For AgentCore Gateway customers, adopting 2026-07-28 is a configuration change. Your AgentCore Gateway can support multiple protocol versions simultaneously. You can add 2026-07-28 to your gateway’s list of &lt;code&gt;supportedVersions&lt;/code&gt; with a single &lt;code&gt;UpdateGateway&lt;/code&gt; call. There’s no need to re-create the gateway or make changes to individual gateway target configurations. The MCP version is a property of the gateway, not of its targets, and updates to that property are performed in place. Tool definitions, target configuration, and inbound authentication (IAM and OAuth) are all unchanged by the version change.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;UpdateGateway&lt;/code&gt; &lt;strong&gt;replaces&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;code&gt;supportedVersions&lt;/code&gt; with the set you send. It does not append. Read the current configuration first, then send the complete list you want the gateway to advertise:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;# 1. Read the current configuration.
aws bedrock-agentcore-control get-gateway \
    --gateway-identifier &amp;lt;gateway-id&amp;gt;

# 2. Advertise 2026-07-28 alongside the existing version.
aws bedrock-agentcore-control update-gateway \
    --name &amp;lt;gateway-name&amp;gt; \
    --role-arn &amp;lt;gateway-role-arn&amp;gt; \
    --protocol-type MCP \
    --authorizer-type &amp;lt;gateway-authorizer-type&amp;gt; \
    --gateway-identifier &amp;lt;gateway-id&amp;gt; \
    --protocol-configuration '{
  "mcp": {
    "supportedVersions": ["2025-11-25", "2026-07-28"]
  }
}'&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AgentCore Gateway works with any of the following protocol versions: 2025-03-26, 2025-06-18, 2025-11-25, and 2026-07-28.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Confirm the new version is live by calling your gateway with the new headers:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;curl -s https://&amp;lt;your-gateway-endpoint&amp;gt;/mcp \
    -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
    -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
    -H "Accept: application/json,text/event-stream" \
    -H "MCP-Protocol-Version: 2026-07-28" \
    -H "Mcp-Method: tools/list" \
    -d '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"tools/list","params":{}}'&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A successful response confirms the gateway served the request at 2026-07-28. A rejection with code &lt;code&gt;-32022&lt;/code&gt; means the version isn’t in the gateway’s &lt;code&gt;supportedVersions&lt;/code&gt; yet, and the response body lists what the gateway currently advertises.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;supportedVersions&lt;/code&gt; is the complete set the gateway advertises, so rolling back is the same operation in reverse. That is, send the list without 2026-07-28. Requests specifying the removed version are rejected with a response body listing the versions the gateway still supports. Make sure clients you’ve already migrated can fall back before you remove the version they’re requesting.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="how-version-selection-works"&gt;How version selection works&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Clients select a version on each request, not through a one-time handshake. Each MCP request declares its version in the &lt;code&gt;MCP-Protocol-Version&lt;/code&gt; header, and the gateway honors it when it’s in &lt;code&gt;supportedVersions&lt;/code&gt;:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A request naming a version the gateway advertises is served in that version. On a dual-version gateway (for example, 2026-07-28 and 2025-11-25), a 2026-07-28 request gets the new behavior and a 2025-11-25 request gets the earlier behavior.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A request naming a version the gateway does &lt;em&gt;not&lt;/em&gt; advertise is rejected with &lt;code&gt;HTTP 400&lt;/code&gt; and code &lt;code&gt;-32022&lt;/code&gt;. The response body lists the versions the gateway supports.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A request that omits the header gets the default version, 2025-03-26. If that default isn’t in the gateway’s &lt;code&gt;supportedVersions&lt;/code&gt;, the request is rejected.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Your existing clients continue to work as long as they request protocol versions listed in the gateway’s &lt;code&gt;supportedVersions&lt;/code&gt;. That gives you a clean, three-stage rollout: add 2026-07-28 alongside your existing versions, migrate clients at your own pace, and only after all clients request the new version, trim the list down.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If your gateway fronts an MCP server target that upgrades to 2026-07-28 before your clients do, it translates between versions, so a &lt;code&gt;2025-*&lt;/code&gt; client can call ordinary tools on a 2026-07-28 target and receive results in the format it expects, without upgrading. However, this translation does not currently support elicitations and sampling calls from servers to clients. If a &lt;code&gt;2025-*&lt;/code&gt; client calls a tool on the &lt;code&gt;2026-*&lt;/code&gt; server that requires elicitation or sampling, it receives an error.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;MCP &lt;code&gt;2026-07-28&lt;/code&gt; is the protocol’s biggest revision ever, and it’s designed to be the last revision that breaks compatibility. Statelessness gives MCP a foundation that scales on commodity HTTP infrastructure while MCP Extensions give new capabilities room to evolve on their own schedules.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Gateway, adding support for the latest protocol version is only one &lt;code&gt;UpdateGateway&lt;/code&gt; call. To get started, see the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/gateway.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Gateway documentation&lt;/a&gt;, review the &lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;MCP 2026-07-28 specification&lt;/a&gt; and its &lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/changelog" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;changelog&lt;/a&gt;, and try enabling the new version on a gateway today. If you have feedback on the specification itself, the maintainers welcome issues in the &lt;a href="https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/issues" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;MCP specification repository&lt;/a&gt;. For questions about AgentCore Gateway, reach out through &lt;a href="https://repost.aws/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS re:Post&lt;/a&gt; or your usual AWS Support channels.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;hr&gt;
